# Plan 11a — The 1.0.1 Offline Build Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build Nutrition Revamp 1.0.1's code offline from Angus's seven decisions of 2026-10-08 — the once-a-minute hybrid in place of the takeover hook, a budgeted `OnTick` queue that serves every player every game minute, the store moved out of global modData into server-local files with pruning, hunger as vanilla's hunger plus the energy term with bulk scaling relief, recipe nutrition conserved, the tooltip hardened, the neighbour mods recorded and the harness route for the mod's own queue — every piece test-first under the lupa host, with no live boot. The live phase (the shoot-out, the release, live acceptance, the documentation) is Plan 11b, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-plan-11b-live-release.md`, which starts only after this plan closes.

**Architecture:** Every new piece of arithmetic is a pure kernel file (`NR_Kernel_Sched.lua`, `NR_Kernel_Json.lua`, `NR_Kernel_Hybrid.lua`, `NR_Kernel_Satiety.lua`) tested test-first under the lupa host inside the 100 % line gate; the server adapters stay read, call, write. The slow minute keeps its one pipeline (`NR.server.minute`); the takeover's `Hook.CalculateStats` handler and its kernel are deleted, and a new last-but-one pipeline step, `writer`, writes the stats once a player-minute after vanilla's rates are zeroed at the server's `OnGameBoot`. The players adapter becomes a budgeted queue that carries unserved players forward and also carries first sight, one-shot store flushes and (for the harness) ghost records. The store keeps its in-memory `NR.server.store.records` table and persists each player to two alternating JSON slot files through `getFileWriter`/`getFileReader`. The golden trace guards every refactor byte for byte and is re-recorded only in the five tasks that name a behaviour change (Tasks 4, 6, 9, 11 and 14).

**Tech Stack:** the mod's Lua (Kahlua subset; `lupa` 2.8 Lua 5.1 host for kernel and shape tests), Python 3 tools (`tools/food_nutrients.py`, `tools/recipe_scan.py`, `tools/release_pack.py`, a new `tools/recipe_conservation.py`), the harness (PZTestKit; no boot in this plan), the claims register and its delta tools, the jar toolchain at `C:\Users\Angus\pz-b42` (`./pz.sh grep|methods|refs|dump`, read-only; read its `WORKSPACE.md` first).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-plan-11-decisions.md`, "Angus's decisions" → "Decisions taken (Angus, 2026-10-08)", items 1–7, argued from the same memo's "Re-basing the draft" (with "What Plan 10c rewrites"), "Refactor follow-ups for Plan 11", "Performance", "Hitching" (with "Ranked refactors for Plan 11"), Decision 6's nine open items and Appendices A–O. This plan and Plan 11b supersede the single-plan draft of 2026-10-08 (never committed) and the earlier draft `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-plan-11-review-fixes-DRAFT.md`, which stays as a record. The neighbour reads are `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-08-neighbour-reads/report-*.md`. Executors read the spec's decisions section and the task's named appendix before starting a task.

## Global Constraints

Everything in Plan 9's Global Constraints holds (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-plan-9-packaging-release.md`), and through it Plans 1–8's. CLAUDE.md §§ 3–6 are binding: the § 3 gates, § 4's register discipline, § 6's process and model policy. `docs/platform/performance.md` (`#budget`, `#measure`, `## Rules`), `docs/platform/lessons.md` `## Rules` and `docs/areas/testing-your-mod.md` are binding. In addition:

- **The build is 42.21** (decision memo item 8, Angus 2026-10-08; ruling 17): Steam moved the install to Build 42.21 at 03:21 on 2026-10-08 (buildid `25485521`; the game prints `version=42.21.0 4a0e9546ec`, the revision the repository calls the jar id, as 42.20.4 printed `version=42.20.4 b0bbce05d5`; the jar file's sha256 prefix is `e1a69eb743`, 65682033 bytes). No 42.20.4 jar was kept, so nothing is diffed: Task R re-reads on 42.21 every jar fact the two plans and the mod rely on before any other task runs. A register row keeps its 42.20.4 evidence and history; a row Task R re-reads says so in its bound. Prose says 42.21; the machine fields the game writes (the fixture's `build`, the dataset's `meta.build`, a page stamp) read `42.21.0`.
- **No live boot after Task R.** Task R's fixture re-provision and its smoke run are this plan's only live sessions (CLAUDE.md § 5 binds them); every other reading here is offline; the live readings, the release and the documentation are Plan 11b's. The harness commit of Task 19 is the one harness change to PZTestKit this plan lands; its smoke test is Plan 11b's first session.
- **Rule 6 (CLAUDE.md § 1, Angus 2026-10-07):** no noticeable hitching at the 60-player design load; per-tick work only where a measurement shows it reduces hitching; a design is judged by its worst server frame and how often that frame comes.
- **Plan 10c's ruling 2 thresholds** (Plan 11b's shoot-out grades the queue built here against them, at N = 60): the 99th-percentile end-to-end period adds at most **20 ms** over the same session's idle arm; the longest engine window adds at most **25 ms**; **no starvation** (no ghost-minute run later than its period); the total on the ghosts' batch-timed ms a minute event is at most **1.1×** the comparison scheduler's; an empty queue's check costs at most **0.1 ms a frame**.
- **The budget (Decision 6 (b)):** per-tick budget = the measured per-player run cost × the queue ÷ the minute's ticks, plus headroom, **floor 15 ms** (#3489, #3490: "the smallest fixed cap that starved none at 60 on this host"), and it scales with a fast clock. The seed per-run cost before any run is timed is **1583 µs** (#3387, `P.work` in play). A 10 ms cap ran 56.75 of 58 a minute event (#3476); a 5 ms cap starved every event (#3477); the drafted ceil(N / ticks) starved 176 over 167 events (#3478).
- **The clock:** a game minute is **6.27 ticks** at DayLength 1 (#3346) and **37.46** at DayLength 4 (#3347); a fast clock fires **at most one** `EveryOneMinute` a tick (#3348).
- **The store:** pruning on by default at **30 real days** since a player was last seen, an operator sandbox option; real time, not game days. A record is about **10877 bytes** in the save's format (#3468); the request leak is #3417–#3419, #3469, #3470; nothing per-player stays under a global modData name. A username whose slot files already hold a record is never migrated from the global table again (ruling 16).
- **The hybrid (Decision 1 (c)):** the seven `ZomboidGlobals` rise keys (#3362) are saved, then set to **`0`, never `nil`** (#3366), in the server's `OnGameBoot` (#3364, #3380); the mode is never read at a client's `OnGameBoot` (#3381); the writer steps by elapsed world age (#3371), runs after the minute's eats (#3383), folds each auto-drink sip into the THIRST target (#3382). A once-a-minute PANIC write keeps PANIC within **1.2556** of its target at DayLength 1 (#3400) — a floor within about **1.26** above a moodle threshold (6, 30, 65, 80; #2369) flickers. TEMPERATURE held within **0.02 °C** at DayLength 1 and **0.04 °C** at the DayLength 4 spacing (#3393). HUNGER and THIRST stay capped at **0.69** and **0.83** (Plan 5 ruling 14). A writer outage stops hunger rather than falling back to vanilla (accepted by Angus).
- **Hunger (Decision 2 (c)):** vanilla's hunger plus the energy term, bulk scaling a meal's applied relief at **β in 0.25–0.5**; the blend's normaliser **r0 = 2.5079** and clamp **[0.25, 4]** (Appendix D). At β 0.25 the light menu is less hungry than vanilla for **111** minutes a day (MAD 0.0348); at β 0.5, **477** (MAD 0.0708); on the main menu every β is option (a). Relief reads the laddered `getHungerChange()` (cooked ×1.3; stale /1.3; rotten /2.2; burnt /3), never the raw `getHungChange`. A record without the scalar seeds **S = 1 − current HUNGER**; only a new record seeds **S = 1**.
- **Heal once (Appendix J, #3413):** heal before each step; the golden trace reproduces **byte for byte** across Task 12 (sha256 `df7806d8…daa9e8b3` at the plan's start).
- **Test-first:** every defect and every new behaviour is written as a failing test, run and seen to FAIL on the pre-change code; the report quotes the failing run's last line. A test that already passes on the pre-change code is a **follow-up pin**, labelled so in the task and in the report, and never counted as the task's red run. A task with no red run is sent back.
- **Kernel files are pure and one statement per line** (`if c then` / statement / `end` on separate lines); `python -m pytest testing/tests/kernel -q` keeps `test_zz_coverage.py` green (100 % of kernel lines). Measured on 2026-10-08: a `for … in pairs(…)` loop whose body ends in an `if … end` leaves that `end` line unexecuted under the Lua 5.1 line hook, whatever the inputs, so such a body calls a kernel helper that holds the `if` (`K.json.member`, `K.store.addExpired`). Kahlua rules everywhere: no `goto`, no `%d` on floats, no `#` on a Java list.
- **Adapters read, call and write:** new arithmetic or branching on numbers goes in a kernel function; an adapter may branch on presence, side and mode.
- **A traced module's `stats` table gains no key** unless its task re-records the golden trace: the trace walks `NR.server.<m>.stats` for `bus.effects, effects, fast, intake, kinetics, metabolism, nutrients, reconcile, store, strength, training, weight` (`testing/tests/kernel/golden_trace.py` `STATS_NAMES`). New counters go on a new table beside it (`S.file.stats`, `KIN.badAge`, `NR.server.writer.stats`).
- **The golden trace** is re-recorded (`python testing/tests/kernel/golden_trace.py --write`) only by Tasks 4, 6, 9, 11 and 14, each in the commit that makes the named behaviour change (and by Task R only if its regenerated food data moves a food the trace eats, Task R Step 6 item 8, naming "the 42.21 food data"), and the task's report lists the first differing leaf of each changed snapshot and why it moved; every other mod task keeps `test_golden_trace.py` green unchanged. Its `printed_count` is **24** at the plan's start, **25** after Task 9 and **16** after Task 14 (measured on a scratch copy of the tree with each task's code applied, 2026-10-08). A task that cannot keep the trace, or whose trace moves in any way its task does not name, stops and reports; it never re-records.
- **One kernel suite at a time.** `test_golden_trace.py` and `test_zz_coverage.py` read the whole working tree, so a kernel-suite run is only valid while no other implementer is editing a `mod/` Lua file or a kernel test in the same tree. Every `mod/` Lua task runs in one serial lane (the "Execution order"); a task outside that lane that runs the kernel suite does so in its own worktree (`isolation: "worktree"`).
- **Every `mod/` edit shifts `repo:mod/` pointers:** the implementer runs `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 python tools/claims_check.py` (the full run) after the edit and lists each `pointer-line` finding (row, old line, new line) in its report; implementers never edit `docs/reference/claims.tsv`; the controller re-anchors at each wave's close in one register commit.
- **The `mod/` gates on every mod commit:** `python tools/mod_lint.py mod/NutritionRevamp` 0 ERROR; `python tools/kahlua_lint.py mod testing/experiments testing/PZTestKit` 0; `python tools/hotpath_lint.py mod` 0; `python tools/science_check.py --scan mod` 0; `python tools/release_pack.py check mod/NutritionRevamp` 0 ERROR; `python -m pytest tools/tests testing/tests -q` at or above the baseline Task R ledgers at its close, with no failure (2637 collected on 2026-10-08, of which 2635 passed: the two failures are `tools/tests/test_food_scan.py::test_real_install_repeated_keys` and `::test_real_install_has_no_anonymous_blocks`, real-install pins that read 2654 and 34691 where they pin 2653 and 34683 — the 42.21 install's scripts; Task R re-pins them to 42.21 and adds its own tests, so its close reads at least 2637 passed and 0 failed), and never a new failure.
- **CRLF test files.** Most kernel shape tests are CRLF in the working tree (`file testing/tests/kernel/*.py`: `test_minute_pipeline.py`, `test_weight_shape.py`, `test_server_reconcile_shape.py`, `test_metabolism_shape.py`, `test_effects_shape.py`, `test_intake_shape.py`, `test_bench.py`, `test_training_shape.py` among them). An edit reads and writes with `newline=''` and keeps each file's endings (CLAUDE.md § 7); an appended block uses the file's own line ending.
- **A generated file is never edited by hand:** `tools/food_nutrients.py --write` and `tools/recipe_conservation.py --write` regenerate; `--check` must be in sync.
- **New evidence is minted through delta files:** a task with a new reading writes `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-08-plan-11a-build/task-<N>-claims-delta.tsv` with provisional ids `T11<NN>.<n>` (Task 2 is `T1102.n`; Task R's block is `T1199.n`; Plan 11b's are `T113<N>.n`, so no two collide, and every one matches `claimslib.PROVISIONAL_ID_RX`, `^T\d+\.\d+$`) and tags its page sentences with them; it dry-runs the delta with `python tools/claims_delta.py apply <delta> --pages <page> [<page> ...] --dry-run` (the delta file first: `--pages` takes every word after it), and the controller applies it at the wave's close (CLAUDE.md § 4). The next free register id at the plan's start is **#3500**.
- **Commits** are pathspec commits: `git add -- <new files>` for new files only, then `git commit -m "<subject>" -- <every touched path>`; never `--amend`, never `git stash`, `checkout --`, `reset`, `restore` or an index-wide command; retry once after two seconds on `.git/index.lock`; no Claude attribution; implementers never push. The one other commit form is the controller's `git cherry-pick` of a worktree task's commits (ruling 1): it replays commits that were themselves pathspec commits, and only the controller runs it, under ruling 1's preconditions.
- **The version stays 1.0.0 in this plan**; Plan 11b's release task sets 1.0.1.
- **Models (CLAUDE.md § 6):** every dispatch names its model (`opus` or `sonnet`) and asks for the exact model id in the return; one on an older version is re-dispatched.

## Rulings this plan takes

Each is written into the ledger as `Ruling: <what> — <why> — cost if wrong: <…>` at Task 0, and the platform ones become lessons rules in Plan 11b's documentation task.

1. **Order and parallelism:** the "Execution order" section below: Task R alone first; then one serial lane for every task that edits `mod/` Lua, a kernel test or a file the kernel host loads; the docs lane beside it; the two pure-kernel tasks and Task 1 in worktrees. A worktree task's commits reach `main` only by the controller's `git cherry-pick`, after that task's `Task N: complete` line — **the one allowed non-pathspec commit form**: it replays commits that were themselves pathspec commits, and only the controller runs it, in its own call, when no shared-tree implementer is mid-commit (no lane-M or shared-tree lane-D implementer active) and `git diff --cached --quiet` exits 0; on `.git/index.lock` it retries once after two seconds; a conflict is aborted (`git cherry-pick --abort`), ledgered and ruled on. A fix round for a worktree task runs in its own fresh worktree, whose first step cherry-picks the task's commits there (its own tree, so CLAUDE.md § 6's shared-worktree rule is not touched). — The golden trace and the coverage gate read the whole tree, so two implementers in one tree make each other's kernel runs meaningless, and the five re-recording tasks write one file; a cherry-pick before the review would land unreviewed code on `main`. — Cost if wrong: a slower build.
2. **The queue (Decision 6 (b); rules #3497, #3444):** one queue of names carried across minutes and never doubled (online players, one-shot tasks, and the harness's extra names); its per-tick budget is `max(15 ms, meanMs × queued ÷ tpm × 1.5)` fixed at each minute event, where `tpm` is the smaller of the last two minutes' tick counts (6 before two minutes are counted, #3346's shortest day), `meanMs` is an EMA (weight 0.2, the harness's) of the timed per-run cost, seeded 1.6 ms (#3387) and floored at 0.1 ms; a tick runs entries until `runCap = max(1, floor(budget ÷ meanMs))` entries or the budget's wall time is spent; with one tick a minute (a fast clock) the formula itself runs the whole queue each tick. — Headroom 1.5 is 15 ms over the 10 ms that ran at the margin (#3476). — Cost if wrong: a starved minute under a heavier load, caught by Plan 11b's starvation count.
3. **First sight, the day close and the respawn ride the queue:** a new sight (a first join, a reconnect, a respawn's new object; #3358–#3360) is marked at the minute event and its first-sight hooks run in the player's own queue slot just before its minute; the 07:00 day close already runs inside each player's minute, so the queue spreads it (#3427); `OnNewGame` resets the record and evicts the username from the online table, and the next minute adopts the new object and fires first sight once. The golden trace moves with it (Task 9): first sight's random draws now interleave with the minute's work, and the respawned `g3`'s first sight is logged. — The draft's store-at-`OnNewGame` was falsified live (#3358). — Cost if wrong: a respawned player's first-sight mirror one minute late.
4. **`resets` counts every `OnNewGame` over an existing record** (0 for a first character), not only a record already marked dead (deviating from draft ruling 5): a death and a respawn inside one game minute leave the record unmarked when `OnNewGame` fires (#3358's order). — Cost if wrong: a character re-created by an admin counts as a respawn.
5. **The hybrid's mode:** `NR.Mode` is read once, in the server's `OnGameBoot`. Mode 1 (Managed, the default) saves then zeroes the seven rise keys and the writer owns HUNGER, THIRST and FATIGUE. Mode 2 (Overlay) leaves the rates vanilla's and vanilla owns HUNGER, THIRST and FATIGUE; the writer still writes the STRESS, UNHAPPINESS and FOOD_SICKNESS floors, PANIC, TEMPERATURE and INTOXICATION, and auto-drink is not captured. A runtime change of the option is logged and takes effect at the next restart; the self-report names the boot-time mode, never the polled one. — Decision 1: a restart-only mode (#3365). — Cost if wrong: an operator who expects a live switch.
6. **The writer is the pipeline's step after `weight` and before `store`:** it reads this minute's targets (effects, fluids, acute, the satiety scalar) after the eats landed (#3383). The Effects step reads the writer's engine reads from the previous minute (a one-minute lag, a limitation string). — Cost if wrong: one minute of lag on the fatigue multipliers.
7. **The sip fold:** the sip is THIRST's fall below the value the writer last wrote, counted only when no intake landed for that player since the last write; the THIRST write is the pool's target less the sip, and the sip lands as water at the next minute (`fluids.autoDrop`, `NUT.LITRES_PER_THIRST`). — #3382 and its inference from #2770, #2939. — Cost if wrong: an auto-drink in the same minute as a manual drink is missed once, then caught the next minute.
8. **The store:** each player is two alternating slot files `NutritionRevamp/<server>/p_<hex>_a.json` and `_b.json` (the newest complete one wins) plus one index `NutritionRevamp/<server>/index.json` of real-time last-seen stamps; a player's slot is written at most once a real minute from its own queue slot, and at departure a one-shot queue task writes the slot and then the index; the index is also written at boot after a migration and with the hourly prune; a pruned player's two slots are **emptied, not deleted** (Lua has no file delete or rename, J2), and an emptied pair reads as no record; the old global table is copied to files, read back, then removed with `ModData.remove`; `ModData.getOrCreate` is never called again. The folder is keyed by the server name, so a world wiped under the same server name finds the old files and every returning player's record — `resets` included — carries over (the README says so, and that an operator deletes the folder by hand for a fresh start). `NR.RecordKeepDays` (integer, 0–3650, default 30, 0 = never) is in real days. — Decision 3; the per-player slot bounds each write to one record. — Cost if wrong: after a hard kill the world rolls back and the files do not (the memo's accepted cost, stated in the README).
9. **β defaults to 0.25**, an operator option `NR.SatietyBulk` (double, 0.25–0.5): the smallest departure from vanilla's calibration that lets bulk matter (Appendix D: 111 minutes less hungry on the light menu, MAD 0.0348; identical to (a) on the main menu). — Cost if wrong: Angus moves the default within the range.
10. **Heal once:** the post-step heals of Nutrients, Metabolism and Effects go (Appendix J's `heal1pre`); a post-step guard re-stamps the fields a later step reads in the same minute (enumerated in Task 12) from their pre-step values; every other step-made NaN waits one minute for the next pre-step heal, the accepted window, named in a limitation string. — #3413 and the H1 review's arithmetic-class finding. — Cost if wrong: a field outside the list carries a NaN for one minute.
11. **Recipe conservation:** a recipe whose re-based outputs carry more than 1.10× their inputs' calories, every line costed by `tools/recipe_scan.py`'s accounting, is fixed — a mapping error in the data, else a craft-time scale of the outputs' four macros on the server from a generated table; one under 0.90× is reported and left (peel, bones and spills are realistic). The client tooltip keeps the unscaled macros in multiplayer (#1074), documented. — Decision 7 item 5. — Cost if wrong: an energy-destroying recipe stays.
12. **Tooltip guards:** the breaker opens after **10** consecutive failures (TooltipLib's figure, `Core.lua:207-247`) and stays open until a reload; a re-entrant render calls the saved original. — Cost if wrong: a transient fault switches the band off until the next session.
13. **The minute context is named `ctx` everywhere** (the adapters' `pipe` parameter is renamed). — Refactor follow-up. — Cost if wrong: none.
14. **Not this plan:** a public rate-source API, recovery fields that yield to other mods, send-on-change, the UX ideas (Decision 7's "left out"); a coarse minute or slow tier (Decision 6 serves every player every minute); caching Java reads (P4 not triggered); the dt biases of `exEma`, `coldH`, `cafMean`, the bruise roll and `fluids.sweatLmin` (under (b) every player runs every game minute; they matter only under a fast clock); the cheaper heal walk and `metabolism/readActivity` (after heal-once, unpriced); stripping ids (Decision 4: keep); the populated-server reading and fake clients (E7, Angus's); real network latency and zombies (Decision 6 open items 4 and 5, Angus's to supply).
15. **Ghost runs are dry in every arm:** the harness wraps its one ghost-run function (`H0.work`) so every ghost minute, whatever scheduler drives it, runs the writer dry (`NR.server.writer.dry`) and skips the store step for ghost names (`NR.server.store.skip`): a ghost sets no stat on its carrier and writes no slot file and no index entry. — Without it the mod arm and the `budget15` arm would carry different work (Plan 11b's shoot-out). — Cost if wrong: a ghost minute costs slightly less than a real one by the skipped `set` calls and file write, the same in both arms.
16. **The migration never overwrites a newer slot:** a username is migrated from the global table only when `F.load` answers nil for it; one with slot files is counted `kept` and left as it is, and the global table is removed once no record failed. — A crash before the world save brings the stale global table back over newer files. — Cost if wrong: none found; a record whose slots are both torn is not re-migrated (it reads as no record and starts fresh, logged).
17. **The build is 42.21, after Task R's re-baseline** (decision memo item 8, Angus 2026-10-08): every jar fact the two plans and the mod rely on is re-read on 42.21 before any build work; a fact that holds gets a bound note, one that moved is re-anchored, one that changed is superseded (CLAUDE.md § 4.4) and every page tagging it moves to the successor; the food and recipe data are re-scanned, the fixtures re-provisioned and a smoke run compared with #3387; CLAUDE.md § 2 names 42.21. A plan task whose jar dependency changed is amended by the controller before it runs. — Players and servers run 42.21, and no 42.20.4 jar was kept to diff. — Cost if wrong: a re-read misses a changed fact the mod relies on; the live acceptance of Plan 11b is the backstop.

## Execution order

Task 0 (controller) opens the workspace. **Task R (the 42.21 re-baseline) runs next and alone:** nothing else is dispatched until its `Task R: complete` line, because it regenerates three shipped `mod/` files, re-provisions both fixtures and boots the game (CLAUDE.md § 5: no live boot overlaps an offline `mod/` edit). Its report lists every later task whose jar dependency changed; the controller amends this plan (and that task's `task-N-amendments.md`) before the task runs, and ledgers each amendment. Then three lanes run beside each other:

- **Lane M (the shared worktree, strictly serial):** Task 3 (Opus) → Task 4 (Opus; re-records) → Task 5 (Sonnet) → Task 6 (Opus; re-records) → Task 7 (Sonnet) → Task 8 (Sonnet) → Task 9 (Opus; re-records) → Task 11 (Opus; re-records; needs Task 10 merged) → Task 12 (Opus) → Task 14 (Opus; re-records; needs Tasks 1, 2 and 13 merged) → Task 15 (Opus; needs Task 2's J1) → Task 16 (Opus) → Task 17 (Opus; needs Task 2's J3) → Task 18 (Sonnet) → Task 19 (Opus; after Task 15: both edit `NR_Server_Writer.lua` and `test_writer_shape.py`). Each task starts when the one before it has its `Task N: complete` line. Every task in this lane runs the kernel suite, so no other implementer edits the shared tree's `mod/` Lua or kernel tests while one runs.
- **Lane D (docs and the jar):** Task 1 (Opus) is dispatched with `isolation: "worktree"`, because it edits `mod/NutritionRevamp/COMPATIBILITY.md` and runs the `mod/` gates, which would read lane M's half-edited files in the shared tree; then Task 2 (Opus) in the shared worktree (it touches no `mod/` file and runs no kernel suite), after Task 1 is merged: both edit `docs/platform/lua-platform.md`.
- **Lane K (pure new kernel files):** Task 10 (Sonnet implementer, Opus reviewer) and Task 13 (Sonnet) are dispatched with `isolation: "worktree"` after Task R; each creates one new kernel file and its test and runs the kernel suite in its own tree.

**Worktree tasks (1, 10 and 13), their reviews and their fix rounds.** `.superpowers/` is gitignored, so a worktree has none of the workspace files. A worktree implementer, and each fix-round implementer, therefore reads its brief and amendments from, and writes its report, delta and any other workspace file to, the main checkout's absolute workspace `C:\Users\Angus\repos\project_zomboid\.superpowers\sdd\2026-10-08-plan-11a-build\` (briefs `task-N-brief.md`, `task-N-amendments.md`, `task-N-fix-K-brief.md`; reports `task-N-report.md`; deltas `task-N-claims-delta.tsv`); every dispatch of a worktree task names that absolute path, and a `<ws>`-relative or `.superpowers/...` path in such a brief is written out absolute. Only the repository paths a task commits are relative to its worktree. A worktree task's implementer commits in its own worktree and reports the commit shas. The reviewer reads them there (`git show <sha>`). A fix round is a fresh implementer dispatched with `isolation: "worktree"` (a fresh worktree off `main`), whose first step is `git cherry-pick <the task's commits, oldest first>` in that worktree — its own tree, never the shared one — and whose fix lands as further pathspec commits there; the scoped re-review reads that worktree. Nothing of a worktree task reaches `main` before its `Task N: complete` line.

**Merging a worktree task (ruling 1):** after `Task N: complete`, the controller cherry-picks the task's commits (the last fix round's worktree holds them all, the task's own first) onto `main`, oldest first, with `git cherry-pick <sha>...` in its own call — the one allowed non-pathspec commit form, because it replays commits that were themselves pathspec commits and only the controller runs it. Preconditions, each checked in its own call first: no lane-M or shared-tree lane-D implementer is active (so none is mid-commit), and `git diff --cached --quiet` exits 0 (nothing staged in the shared index). On `.git/index.lock` it retries once after two seconds; a conflict is aborted with `git cherry-pick --abort`, ledgered and ruled on before anything else merges. The second precondition can hold Task 10's or Task 13's merge until lane D's Task 2 (a shared-tree implementer) finishes; that wait is accepted, because Task 11 and Task 14 start only after their merges anyway. Then it runs `python -m pytest testing/tests/kernel -q` in a separate call (green, the golden unchanged) before the next lane-M dispatch. The merge happens only between two lane-M tasks. Task 10 merges before Task 11 starts; Task 13 before Task 14; Task 1 before Task 2 starts and before Task 14.

The parallelism is therefore small: three implementers at most (one lane-M task, Task 1 or 2, and Task 10 or 13), and only while lane M runs Tasks 3–9. The controller re-anchors pointer shifts and applies deltas at the wave closes it ledgers (at Task R's close, after Task 9, after Task 15, at Task 20); reviews are read-only and use the tier named in each task's heading.

### Task R: The 42.21 re-baseline — Opus implementer, Opus reviewer

After Task 0 and before every other task; nothing else is dispatched while it runs. It re-reads on 42.21 every jar fact the two plans and the shipped mod rely on, re-scans the food and recipe data, re-provisions the fixtures, smoke-tests the harness and the mod on 42.21 and moves CLAUDE.md § 2 to the new build. The controller dispatches it in two rounds, each a fresh Opus implementer with its own report (`task-R-a-report.md`, `task-R-b-report.md`): **R-a (offline): Steps 1–6**; **R-b (live and close): Steps 7–11**, after R-a's commits and before any `mod/` edit by anyone. One Opus review covers both rounds (its package is `git show -U8` of every Task R commit, artifact JSON excluded), so R-a's commits would be unreviewed when R-b boots unless the next sentence is followed: **the controller reviews R-a first** (a fresh read-only Opus reviewer on `git show -U8` of R-a's commits, before R-b is dispatched, any FAIL fixed by a fresh implementer and re-reviewed in scope; the ledger records the review as R-a's), and the closing review then covers R-b's commits and re-samples R-a's; its register work follows CLAUDE.md § 4 (deltas, the controller mints, owner sentences with their rows) and its live work § 5. `<ws>` below is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-08-plan-11a-build`.

It collects every register id that Plans 11a and 11b cite (each with the task that cites it), the spec's argued sections cite (from `## The spikes` to `## Appendices`, row ranges like `#3362–#3373` expanded), the shipped mod cites (every file under `mod/`), and the area pages' `## Rules` cite — plus, one level deep, the ids a `rule` row's bound names (`inference; a reading of <ids>`) — follows each superseded id to its live successor, and writes `<ws>/task-R-inventory.tsv` (id, status, grade, the pointer forms, cited by) and `<ws>/task-R-ids.txt` (the live ids with a `jar:` or `lua:` pointer, one per line, with what cites them after a tab):

```python
"""Task R's inventory (Plan 11a): every register row the two plans, the spec's argued sections, the shipped mod
and the area pages' rules rely on. Run from the repository root."""
import glob
import os
import re
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, "tools")
import claimslib as cl  # noqa: E402

WS = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ID = re.compile(r"#(\d{4})(?!\d)")
RANGE = re.compile(r"#(\d{4})\s*[\u2013-]\s*#(\d{4})")
CITED = {}


def note(cid, why):
    CITED.setdefault(cid, set()).add(why)


def ids_in(text):
    out = set("#" + m for m in ID.findall(text))
    for a, b in RANGE.findall(text):
        if int(b) - int(a) < 200:
            out |= {"#%04d" % n for n in range(int(a), int(b) + 1)}
    return out


for plan in ("docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-plan-11a-build.md",
             "docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-plan-11b-live-release.md"):
    tag = os.path.basename(plan)[11:19]
    task = "constraints"
    for line in open(plan, encoding="utf-8"):
        m = re.match(r"### Task (\w+)", line)
        if m:
            task = m.group(1)
        for cid in ids_in(line):
            note(cid, "%s Task %s" % (tag, task))

spec = open("docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-plan-11-decisions.md", encoding="utf-8").read()
argued = spec[spec.index("## The spikes"):spec.index("## Appendices")]
for cid in ids_in(argued):
    note(cid, "spec")

for path in glob.glob("mod/**/*", recursive=True):
    if os.path.isfile(path) and not path.endswith(".png"):
        for cid in ids_in(open(path, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").read()):
            note(cid, "mod:" + path.replace("\\", "/").split("/")[-1])

for page in glob.glob("docs/areas/*.md"):
    m = re.search(r"^## Rules\s*$(.*?)^## ", open(page, encoding="utf-8").read(), re.M | re.S)
    if m:
        for cid in ids_in(m.group(1)):
            note(cid, "rules:" + os.path.basename(page))

rows = {r["id"]: r for r in cl.read_register("docs/reference/claims.tsv")}
for cid in list(CITED):
    r = rows.get(cid)
    if r is not None and r["kind"] == "rule" and r["bound"].startswith("inference"):
        for dep in ids_in(r["bound"]):
            note(dep, "rule-dep:" + cid)


def live(cid):
    seen = set()
    while cid in rows and rows[cid]["status"] == "superseded" and cid not in seen:
        seen.add(cid)
        cid = rows[cid]["successor"].split(",")[0].strip()
    return cid


inv, jar_ids = [], []
for cid in sorted(CITED):
    if cid not in rows:
        inv.append([cid, "no-row", "", "", "; ".join(sorted(CITED[cid]))])
        continue
    lid = live(cid)
    r = rows[lid]
    try:
        forms = sorted({f for f, _t in cl.parse_pointers(r["pointer"])})
    except ValueError:
        forms = ["unparsed"]
    why = "; ".join(sorted(CITED[cid])) + ("" if lid == cid else "; via " + cid)
    inv.append([lid, r["status"], r["grade"], ",".join(forms), why])
    if ("jar" in forms or "lua" in forms) and r["status"] != "superseded" and lid not in jar_ids:
        jar_ids.append(lid)

with open(os.path.join(WS, "task-R-inventory.tsv"), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
    f.write("id\tstatus\tgrade\tforms\tcited_by\n")
    for row in inv:
        f.write("\t".join(row) + "\n")
with open(os.path.join(WS, "task-R-ids.txt"), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
    for cid in jar_ids:
        f.write("%s\t%s\n" % (cid, "; ".join(sorted(CITED.get(cid, ["via a superseded id"])))))
print("inventory: %d ids, %d with a jar: or lua: pointer" % (len(inv), len(jar_ids)))
```

  Paste the printed line into the report, with the count of rows by grade and by form (run against the tree while revising this plan it printed `inventory: 887 ids, 485 with a jar: or lua: pointer`, and the tool of Step 3 read `#0001` and `#3326`'s seven jar pointers as `shifted` in about a minute); the `run:` (M) rows are measured on 42.20.4 and are re-read only where Step 8 re-measures them (#3387, #3346), and the report lists the rest as "42.20.4 measurements, not re-read"; the `data:` rows go to Step 6.
- [ ] **Step 3: `tools/jar_recheck.py`, test-first.** Write `tools/tests/test_jar_recheck.py`:

```python
"""tools/jar_recheck.py (Plan 11a Task R): pointer parsing, the dump's line table, the verdicts, the report."""
import os
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
import claimslib as cl  # noqa: E402
import jar_recheck as jr  # noqa: E402

DUMP = """### Eat(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;F)Z
      0  L5728  aload_0          
      1         aload_1          
      4         invokevirtual    zombie/characters/IsoGameCharacter.Eat
      7         ireturn          

### Eat(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;FZ)Z
      0  L5740  aload_1          
     28  L5760  aload            4
     30         fload_2          
     78         fstore_2         
     79  L5764  return           
"""


def test_parse_a_span_pointer():
    p = jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@78 L5753-L5757")
    assert (p["cls"], p["meth"], p["desc"], p["spans"], p["lines"]) == (
        "IsoGameCharacter", "Eat", None, [(28, 78, 5753, 5757)], None)


def test_parse_the_other_forms():
    assert jr.parse_jar("Food.getBaseHunger()F @0 L1891")["desc"] == "()F"
    assert jr.parse_jar("Food.update @38\u2013@46 L369-370")["spans"] == [(38, 46, 369, 370)]
    assert jr.parse_jar("Food.getHungerChange()F @L1682-L1708")["lines"] == (1682, 1708)
    assert jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter$XP.AddXP @0 L14183")["cls"] == "IsoGameCharacter$XP"
    assert jr.parse_jar("zombie/characters/Stats method list (34 entries)") is None
    assert jr.parse_jar("SandboxOptions.set(String,Object)") is None


def test_the_dump_carries_lines_forward_per_overload():
    ov = jr.parse_dump(DUMP)
    assert [d for d, _o, _i in ov] == ["(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;F)Z",
                                       "(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;FZ)Z"]
    offs = ov[1][1]
    assert offs[28] == 5760 and offs[30] == 5760 and offs[78] == 5760 and offs[79] == 5764
    assert ov[1][2][28].startswith("28  L5760  aload")


def test_descriptor_matching():
    assert jr.desc_matches(None, "(F)Z")
    assert jr.desc_matches("(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;F)", "(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;F)Z")
    assert jr.desc_matches("(InventoryItem,float)Z", "(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;F)Z")
    assert not jr.desc_matches("(InventoryItem,float)Z", "(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;FZ)Z")
    assert jr.simple_params("([ILjava/lang/String;J)V") == ["int[]", "String", "long"]


def test_the_verdicts():
    ov = jr.parse_dump(DUMP)
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@78 L5760"), ov) == ("same", None)
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@79 L5753-L5757"), ov) == (
        "shifted", "IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@79 L5760-L5764")
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @29 L5760"), ov)[0] == "offsets-missing"
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat(I)Z @0 L5728"), ov)[0] == "no-method"
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat(InventoryItem,float)Z @0 L5728"), ov) == ("same", None)
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @L5740-L5764"), ov) == ("same", None)
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @L5741-L5764"), ov)[0] == "lines-missing"
    assert jr.check_jar(jr.parse_jar("IsoGameCharacter.Eat @0 L1"), jr.parse_dump("method not found: Eat"))[0] == "no-method"


def test_a_lua_anchor_same_moved_missing(tmp_path):
    d = tmp_path / "lua" / "client"
    d.mkdir(parents=True)
    (d / "A.lua").write_text("x = 1\nif not isClient() then\nend\n", encoding="utf-8")
    m = str(tmp_path)
    assert jr.check_lua('lua/client/A.lua:2 "if not isClient() then"', m) == ("same", None)
    assert jr.check_lua('lua/client/A.lua:1 "if not isClient() then"', m) == (
        "anchor-moved", 'lua/client/A.lua:2 "if not isClient() then"')
    assert jr.check_lua('lua/client/A.lua:1 "gone()"', m) == ("anchor-missing", None)
    assert jr.check_lua('lua/client/B.lua:1 "x"', m) == ("no-file", None)


def test_main_writes_one_line_per_pointer(tmp_path, monkeypatch, capsys):
    base = dict.fromkeys(cl.COLUMNS, "x")
    a = dict(base, id="#0001", pointer='jar:IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@79 L5753-L5757; lua:lua/client/A.lua:2 "y = 2"')
    b = dict(base, id="#0002", pointer="jar:Nope.x @0 L1")
    reg = tmp_path / "claims.tsv"
    cl.write_register(str(reg), [a, b])
    (tmp_path / "ids.txt").write_text("#0001\tcited by Task 14\n#0002\n#0001\n", encoding="utf-8")
    lua = tmp_path / "media" / "lua" / "client"
    lua.mkdir(parents=True)
    (lua / "A.lua").write_text("y = 2\n", encoding="utf-8")
    monkeypatch.setattr(jr, "class_index", lambda jar: {"IsoGameCharacter": ["zombie/characters/IsoGameCharacter"]})
    monkeypatch.setattr(jr, "dump_text", lambda c, m, jar, pzdis: DUMP)
    out = tmp_path / "report.tsv"
    assert jr.main(["--ids", str(tmp_path / "ids.txt"), "--out", str(out), "--register", str(reg),
                    "--media", str(tmp_path / "media")]) == 0
    lines = out.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
    assert lines[0].split("\t") == list(jr.HEADER)
    assert [l.split("\t")[:5] for l in lines[1:]] == [
        ["#0001", "jar", "IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@79 L5753-L5757", "shifted", "IsoGameCharacter.Eat @28-@79 L5760-L5764"],
        ["#0001", "lua", 'lua/client/A.lua:2 "y = 2"', "anchor-moved", 'lua/client/A.lua:1 "y = 2"'],
        ["#0002", "jar", "Nope.x @0 L1", "no-class", ""]]
    assert "3 pointers over 2 ids" in capsys.readouterr().out
```

  Run `python -m pytest tools/tests/test_jar_recheck.py -q` — FAIL (no module). Quote. Then write `tools/jar_recheck.py` (standard library only, as every tool here):

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Re-read register pointers on the installed build (Plan 11a Task R, the 42.21 re-baseline).

For every `jar:` pointer of the form `Class[$Inner].method[(params)[ret]] @a[-@b] La[-Lb]` (several spans allowed)
or `Class.method @La-Lb` (lines only), it dumps the method with the jar toolchain's disassembler
(C:\\Users\\Angus\\pz-b42\\tools\\pzdis.py, what pz.sh runs; read-only) and reports whether each cited offset is
still an instruction whose source line is the cited line. For every `lua:` pointer `<path under media/>:<line>
"anchor"` it reports whether that line of the install's file still carries the anchor. It writes one TSV line per
pointer with a status, a proposed re-anchored pointer when every offset or the anchor is still there but the lines
moved, and the instructions at the cited offsets, so a reader can judge the claim. It never edits the register:
whether a claim holds is the reader's verdict.

Statuses: same, shifted, offsets-missing, lines-missing, no-method, no-class, ambiguous-class, unparsed (jar);
same, anchor-moved, anchor-missing, anchor-ambiguous, no-file, unparsed (lua); no-row.

    python tools/jar_recheck.py --ids <file of #dddd ids> --out <report.tsv>
                                [--register TSV] [--jar JAR] [--pzdis PZDIS] [--media DIR]
"""
import argparse
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import zipfile

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import claimslib as cl  # noqa: E402

REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
REGISTER = os.path.join(REPO, "docs", "reference", "claims.tsv")
JAR = r"D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\ProjectZomboid\projectzomboid.jar"
MEDIA = r"D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\ProjectZomboid\media"
PZDIS = r"C:\Users\Angus\pz-b42\tools\pzdis.py"
HEADER = ("id", "form", "pointer", "status", "proposed", "span")

JAR_PTR_RX = re.compile(r"^(?P<cls>[A-Za-z_][\w$]*)\.(?P<meth>[\w<>$]+)(?P<desc>\([^)]*\)\S*)?\s+(?P<spans>@.*)$")
SPAN_RX = re.compile(r"@(?P<o1>\d+)(?:\s*[-\u2013]\s*@(?P<o2>\d+))?\s+L(?P<l1>\d+)(?:\s*[-\u2013]\s*L?(?P<l2>\d+))?")
LINES_RX = re.compile(r"^@L(?P<l1>\d+)(?:\s*[-\u2013]\s*L?(?P<l2>\d+))?$")
HEAD_RX = re.compile(r"^### (?P<name>[^(\s]+)(?P<desc>\(.*)$")
INSN_RX = re.compile(r"^\s+(?P<off>\d+)\s+(?:L(?P<line>\d+)\s+)?(?P<op>\S+)")
LUA_RX = re.compile(r'^(?P<path>[^\s"]+):(?P<l1>\d+)(?:[-\u2013](?P<l2>\d+))?\s+"(?P<anchor>[^"]+)"$')
PRIM = {"Z": "boolean", "B": "byte", "C": "char", "S": "short", "I": "int", "J": "long", "F": "float",
        "D": "double", "V": "void"}


def parse_jar(text):
    """A jar pointer's payload -> {text, cls, meth, desc, spans: [(o1, o2, l1, l2)], lines: (l1, l2) | None},
    or None for a form this tool does not read (a member list, a bare signature, a dotted class path)."""
    text = text.strip()
    m = JAR_PTR_RX.match(text)
    if m is None:
        return None
    rest = m.group("spans").strip()
    out = {"text": text, "cls": m.group("cls"), "meth": m.group("meth"), "desc": m.group("desc"),
           "spans": [], "lines": None}
    lm = LINES_RX.match(rest)
    if lm is not None:
        l1 = int(lm.group("l1"))
        out["lines"] = (l1, int(lm.group("l2") or l1))
        return out
    for sm in SPAN_RX.finditer(rest):
        out["spans"].append((int(sm.group("o1")), int(sm.group("o2")) if sm.group("o2") else None,
                             int(sm.group("l1")), int(sm.group("l2")) if sm.group("l2") else None))
    if not out["spans"] or SPAN_RX.sub("", rest).strip(" ,;"):
        return None
    return out


def parse_dump(text):
    """The disassembler's dump -> [(desc, {offset: line}, {offset: instruction text})], one per overload; each
    offset maps to the source line in force at it (the line table carried forward)."""
    out = []
    offs = insn = None
    line = None
    for raw in text.splitlines():
        h = HEAD_RX.match(raw)
        if h is not None:
            offs, insn, line = {}, {}, None
            out.append((h.group("desc"), offs, insn))
            continue
        if offs is None:
            continue
        i = INSN_RX.match(raw)
        if i is not None:
            if i.group("line"):
                line = int(i.group("line"))
            offs[int(i.group("off"))] = line
            insn[int(i.group("off"))] = raw.strip()
    return out


def simple_params(desc):
    """'(Lzombie/inventory/InventoryItem;FZ)Z' -> ['InventoryItem', 'float', 'boolean']."""
    inner = desc[1:desc.index(")")]
    out, i = [], 0
    while i < len(inner):
        dims = 0
        while inner[i] == "[":
            dims += 1
            i += 1
        if inner[i] == "L":
            j = inner.index(";", i)
            name = inner[i + 1:j].rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
            i = j + 1
        else:
            name = PRIM[inner[i]]
            i += 1
        out.append(name + "[]" * dims)
    return out


def desc_matches(cited, actual):
    """A cited `(...)` names an overload by its raw descriptor's prefix or by its simple parameter names."""
    if cited is None:
        return True
    if actual.startswith(cited):
        return True
    names = [p.strip() for p in cited[1:cited.index(")")].split(",") if p.strip()]
    return names == simple_params(actual)


def reanchor(text, offs):
    """The pointer text with each span's L numbers replaced by the lines now at its offsets."""
    def fix(sm):
        s, base = sm.group(0), sm.start(0)
        n1 = offs[int(sm.group("o1"))]
        head = s[:sm.start("l1") - base] + str(n1)
        if sm.group("l2") is None:
            return head + s[sm.end("l1") - base:]
        n2 = offs[int(sm.group("o2"))] if sm.group("o2") else n1
        return head + s[sm.end("l1") - base:sm.start("l2") - base] + str(n2) + s[sm.end("l2") - base:]
    return SPAN_RX.sub(fix, text)


def check_jar(ptr, overloads):
    """(status, proposed pointer or None) for one parsed jar pointer against the method's overloads."""
    cands = [(d, offs) for d, offs, _i in overloads if desc_matches(ptr["desc"], d)]
    if not cands:
        return "no-method", None
    if ptr["lines"] is not None:
        l1, l2 = ptr["lines"]
        for _d, offs in cands:
            have = set(v for v in offs.values() if v is not None)
            if l1 in have and l2 in have:
                return "same", None
        return "lines-missing", None
    best = None
    for _d, offs in cands:
        if not all(o in offs for o1, o2, _a, _b in ptr["spans"] for o in (o1, o2) if o is not None):
            continue
        if all(offs[o1] == l1 and (o2 is None or offs[o2] == (l2 if l2 is not None else l1))
               for o1, o2, l1, l2 in ptr["spans"]):
            return "same", None
        if best is None:
            best = offs
    if best is None:
        return "offsets-missing", None
    return "shifted", reanchor(ptr["text"], best)


def span_text(ptr, overloads):
    """The instructions at the cited offsets in the first overload that holds them all, for the reader."""
    for d, _offs, insn in overloads:
        if desc_matches(ptr["desc"], d) and all(o1 in insn for o1, _o2, _a, _b in ptr["spans"]):
            parts = []
            for o1, o2, _a, _b in ptr["spans"]:
                parts.append(insn[o1])
                if o2 is not None and o2 in insn:
                    parts.append(insn[o2])
            return " | ".join(parts)
    return ""


def check_lua(text, media):
    """(status, proposed pointer or None) for one `lua:` payload against the install's media folder."""
    m = LUA_RX.match(text.strip())
    if m is None:
        return "unparsed", None
    path = os.path.join(media, *m.group("path").split("/"))
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        return "no-file", None
    lines = open(path, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").read().split("\n")
    n, anchor = int(m.group("l1")), m.group("anchor")
    if 1 <= n <= len(lines) and anchor in lines[n - 1]:
        return "same", None
    hits = [k + 1 for k, l in enumerate(lines) if anchor in l]
    if not hits:
        return "anchor-missing", None
    if len(hits) > 1:
        return "anchor-ambiguous", None
    span = str(hits[0])
    if m.group("l2"):
        span += "-%d" % (int(m.group("l2")) + hits[0] - n)
    return "anchor-moved", '%s:%s "%s"' % (m.group("path"), span, anchor)


def class_index(jar):
    """{short class name: [internal names]} over every class in the jar."""
    idx = {}
    with zipfile.ZipFile(jar) as z:
        for name in z.namelist():
            if name.endswith(".class"):
                idx.setdefault(name[:-6].rsplit("/", 1)[-1], []).append(name[:-6])
    return idx


def resolve(short, idx):
    names = idx.get(short, [])
    if len(names) > 1:
        game = [n for n in names if n.startswith("zombie/") or n.startswith("se/krka/")]
        if len(game) == 1:
            return game
    return names


def dump_text(internal, meth, jar=JAR, pzdis=PZDIS):
    res = subprocess.run([sys.executable, pzdis, jar, "dump", internal, meth], capture_output=True, text=True,
                         encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    return res.stdout


def recheck(ids, rows, idx, dumper, media):
    by_id = {r["id"]: r for r in rows}
    out, cache = [], {}
    for cid in ids:
        r = by_id.get(cid)
        if r is None:
            out.append([cid, "", "", "no-row", "", ""])
            continue
        try:
            ptrs = cl.parse_pointers(r["pointer"])
        except ValueError:
            out.append([cid, "", r["pointer"], "unparsed", "", ""])
            continue
        for form, text in ptrs:
            if form == "lua":
                st, new = check_lua(text, media)
                out.append([cid, form, text, st, new or "", ""])
                continue
            if form != "jar":
                continue
            p = parse_jar(text)
            if p is None:
                out.append([cid, form, text, "unparsed", "", ""])
                continue
            names = resolve(p["cls"], idx)
            if not names:
                out.append([cid, form, text, "no-class", "", ""])
                continue
            if len(names) > 1:
                out.append([cid, form, text, "ambiguous-class", "", " ".join(names)])
                continue
            key = (names[0], p["meth"])
            if key not in cache:
                cache[key] = parse_dump(dumper(names[0], p["meth"]))
            st, new = check_jar(p, cache[key])
            out.append([cid, form, text, st, new or "", span_text(p, cache[key])])
    return out


def main(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    ap.add_argument("--ids", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--register", default=REGISTER)
    ap.add_argument("--jar", default=JAR)
    ap.add_argument("--pzdis", default=PZDIS)
    ap.add_argument("--media", default=MEDIA)
    a = ap.parse_args(argv)
    ids = []
    for line in open(a.ids, encoding="utf-8"):
        m = re.match(r"\s*(#\d{4})", line)
        if m and m.group(1) not in ids:
            ids.append(m.group(1))
    out = recheck(ids, cl.read_register(a.register), class_index(a.jar),
                  lambda c, m: dump_text(c, m, a.jar, a.pzdis), a.media)
    with open(a.out, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
        f.write("\t".join(HEADER) + "\n")
        for row in out:
            f.write("\t".join(c.replace("\t", " ") for c in row) + "\n")
    counts = {}
    for row in out:
        counts[row[3]] = counts.get(row[3], 0) + 1
    print("jar_recheck: %d pointers over %d ids: %s" % (
        len(out), len(ids), ", ".join("%s %d" % kv for kv in sorted(counts.items()))))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

Paste its summary line. Then for each row, the verdict, with the reader's evidence in the report:

A `status` delta row (status: the row's current status, unchanged; a re-read never settles an `open` row, and three inventory rows are `open`: they keep `open`, with this bound note, and CLAUDE.md § 4.3 alone governs settling them) whose bound is the row's current bound byte for byte followed by `; re-read on 42.21 (buildid 25485521), holds`.

The delta row carries the re-anchored pointer cell (every other pointer of the row unchanged) and the bound `<current bound>; re-read on 42.21 (buildid 25485521), holds; re-anchored from <the old jar or lua pointer>`. A `same` row cited only by the mod, the spec or a rule may rest on the tool's match and its printed span; the report says which rows were read by hand.

```python
"""Task R: what the 42.21 scan changed against the 42.20.4 one, item by item and recipe by recipe."""
import json
import os

WS = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
FIELDS = ("kind", "calories", "carbohydrates", "lipids", "proteins", "hunger_change", "thirst_change",
          "unhappy_change", "boredom_change", "days_fresh", "days_totally_rotten", "nutrition_basis", "fluid_ids",
          "fluid_capacity", "replace_on_use", "replace_on_deplete")


def load(path, key, name):
    rows = json.load(open(path, encoding="utf-8"))[key]
    return {r[name]: r for r in rows}


old = load(os.path.join(WS, "food-items-42.20.4.json"), "items", "id")
new = load("data/food-items.json", "items", "id")
print("foods: %d -> %d" % (len(old), len(new)))
print("added:", sorted(set(new) - set(old)))
print("removed:", sorted(set(old) - set(new)))
for i in sorted(set(old) & set(new)):
    moved = ["%s %r -> %r" % (f, old[i].get(f), new[i].get(f)) for f in FIELDS if old[i].get(f) != new[i].get(f)]
    if moved:
        print("changed", i, "; ".join(moved))
ro = load(os.path.join(WS, "recipes-42.20.4.json"), "recipes", "name")
rn = load("data/recipes.json", "recipes", "name")
print("recipes: %d -> %d" % (len(ro), len(rn)))
print("added:", sorted(set(rn) - set(ro)))
print("removed:", sorted(set(ro) - set(rn)))
for n in sorted(set(ro) & set(rn)):
    if ro[n].get("inputs") != rn[n].get("inputs") or ro[n].get("outputs") != rn[n].get("outputs"):
        print("changed recipe", n)
```

### Task 2: The jar reads that gate design (J1–J4) — Opus implementer, Opus reviewer

Lane D, in the shared worktree, after Task 1 is merged: both edit `docs/platform/lua-platform.md`, and this task writes below Task 1's two sentences. It edits no `mod/` file and runs no kernel suite. The reads are on 42.21 (ruling 17); the re-reads of the rows the spec's decisions rest on are Task R's, and its report (and the amendments it calls for) are read before this task starts. A jar fact these reads touch that Task R superseded is cited by its successor.

**Files:**
- Create (workspace): `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-08-plan-11a-build/task-2-jar-memo.md`, `task-2-claims-delta.tsv`
- Modify: `docs/facts/eating-pipeline.md` (J1), `docs/platform/lua-platform.md` (J2, J4; below Task 1's two sentences), `docs/areas/eat-and-cook-hooks.md` (J3)

**Interfaces:**

```python
from .server_host import Host


def test_a_failed_clock_read_never_empties_the_stomach_on_the_next_minute():
    h = Host()
    p = h.player("admin")
    h.online(p)
    h.T.age = 500.0
    h.minute(); h.tick(5)                       # first sight and one worked minute at age 500
    fill0 = h.record("admin").stomachFill
    good = h.G.getGameTime
    h.G.getGameTime = h.rt.eval("function() error('clock') end")
    h.minute(); h.tick(5)                       # a minute whose clock read fails
    h.G.getGameTime = good
    h.T.age = 500.0 + 1 / 60
    h.minute(); h.tick(5)                       # the next good minute: one game minute later
    assert h.record("admin").kineticsAge == 500.0 + 1 / 60
    assert h.record("admin").stomachFill > fill0 - 0.01   # one minute of emptying, not 500 hours
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The raise log.** In `NR_Core.lua` replace the bodies of `NR.num`, `NR.obj` and `NR.flag` and add the helper above them:

```lua
-- The raise log (Plan 11 Task 3, the bug review's should-fix): a member that raises inside NR.num, NR.obj or
-- NR.flag is logged once per member name per Lua state, so a broken getter is visible without a line a minute.
NR.raised = {}
function NR.noteRaise(name, err)
    if NR.raised[name] ~= nil then return end
    NR.raised[name] = true
    NR.log.say(2, "call: " .. tostring(name) .. " raised: " .. tostring(err))
end

function NR.num(o, name, dflt, ...)
    local ok, present, v = pcall(NR.call, o, name, ...)
    if not ok then NR.noteRaise(name, present) end
    if ok and present and NR.finite(v) then return v end
    return dflt
end

function NR.obj(o, name, ...)
    local ok, present, v = pcall(NR.call, o, name, ...)
    if not ok then NR.noteRaise(name, present) end
    if ok and present then return v end
    return nil
end

function NR.flag(o, name, ...)
    local ok, present, v = pcall(NR.call, o, name, ...)
    if not ok then NR.noteRaise(name, present) end
    return ok and present and v == true
end
```

```lua
function IN.assemble(b, rawAfter, lookup, thirstAfter, templates, info)
    local trace = IN.newTrace()

end
```

```python
def test_the_push_signature_moves_with_any_live_class(host):
    s0 = host.call("view.pushSignature", 1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
    assert host.call("view.pushSignature", 1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0) == s0
    for args in ((1.6, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0), (1.0, 5.0, 0.0, 0.0), (1.0, 0.0, 400.0, 0.0), (1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 30.0)):

def test_the_push_signature_reads_nil_as_zero(host):

def test_the_push_offset_is_inside_the_gap_and_differs_by_name(host):
    a = host.call("view.pushOffset", "admin", 60000)
    b = host.call("view.pushOffset", "bob", 60000)

    assert host.call("view.pushOffset", "admin", 60000) == a
    assert host.call("view.pushOffset", "", 60000) == 0
```

```python
from .server_host import Host

SEND = r"""
NR_T.sent = {}
sendServerCommand = function(p, module, cmd, m) NR_T.sent[#NR_T.sent + 1] = cmd end
getTimestampMs = function() return NR_T.now end

def online(h, name="a"):

def test_mirror_requests_inside_the_gap_are_denied():

    p = online(h)
    n0 = len(h.T.sent)

        h.fire("OnClientCommand", "NutritionRevamp", "mirror.request", p, None)
    assert len(h.T.sent) - n0 == 1
    assert h.NR.server.bus.requests.stats.denied == 4
    h.T.now = h.T.now + 5001
    h.fire("OnClientCommand", "NutritionRevamp", "mirror.request", p, None)

def test_a_class_change_marks_the_player_for_a_push():

    B = h.NR.server.bus

        h.T.age = h.T.age + 1 / 60

    h.T.age = h.T.age + 1 / 60
    h.minute(); h.tick(25)
    h.T.now = h.T.now + 60001
    h.T.age = h.T.age + 1 / 60
    h.minute(); h.tick(25)                               # the mark made in one minute goes out with the next flush
    assert B.effects.stats.pushes >= n0 + 1


def test_two_players_first_seen_together_push_at_different_wall_times():
    h = Host(extra_env=SEND)
    a, b = h.player("admin"), h.player("bob")            # offsets 8475 and 37717 ms inside the 60 s gap

    B = h.NR.server.bus

    assert "admin" in first and "bob" in first and abs(first["admin"] - first["bob"]) >= 20000
```

```lua
-- The live class rungs as one integer (Plan 11 Task 4; the draft's ruling 8): a change in any of energy, hydration,
-- stimulant or sleep moves it. Deficiency and excess move with the nutrient epoch, which already marks a push. nil
-- reads 0.
function K.view.pushSignature(energyState, dehydPct, caf, debtH)
    local e = K.view.rung(energyState or 0, K.view.ENERGY_AT)
    local hy = K.view.rung(dehydPct or 0, K.view.HYDRATION_AT)
    local s = K.view.rung(caf or 0, K.view.STIMULANT_AT)
    local sl = K.view.rung(debtH or 0, K.view.SLEEP_AT)
    return e * 1000 + hy * 100 + s * 10 + sl
end

-- A player's push phase inside the gap (Plan 11 Task 4; rule #3498): a rolling hash of the username modulo the gap,
-- so players first seen together push in different frames ever after (#3456).
function K.view.pushOffset(username, gapMs)
    local h = 0
    for i = 1, string.len(username) do
        h = (h * 31 + string.byte(username, i)) % 1000003
    end
    return h % gapMs
end
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement the adapter.** In `NR_Server_Bus.lua`: the state gains `REQUEST_GAP_MS = 5000`, `requests = { last = {}, stats = { answered = 0, denied = 0 } }`, `lastSig = {}`, `build = { failed = 0 }`; move `nowMs` above `B.sendMirror`; `B.sendMirror` becomes

```lua
function B.sendMirror(player, record)
    if player == nil or record == nil then return false end
    if sendServerCommand == nil then return false end
    local okB, m = pcall(K.mirror.build, record, B.meta(), NR.data and NR.data.records and NR.data.records.ORDER)
    if not okB then
        B.build.failed = B.build.failed + 1
        NR.log.say(2, "bus: mirror build failed for " .. tostring(record.username) .. ": " .. tostring(m))
        return false
    end
    local ok = pcall(sendServerCommand, player, B.module, "mirror", m)
    if not ok then NR.log.say(2, "bus: sendServerCommand failed for " .. tostring(record.username)) end
    return ok
end
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the kernel.** In `NR_Kernel_Vector.lua`, remove `K.vector.meat` and its comment (its only caller is `K.intake.assemble`: `grep -rn "vector.meat" mod testing tools` lists it and the tests you replaced) and add:

```lua
-- The instance scale (Plan 11 Task 6; Appendix E, #3326-#3340): a table, declared or craft vector is scaled to the
-- calories the eat delivered, b.cal x frac over the vector's own calories, with factor 1 -- the macros' own scale,
-- right for a plain, partial, caught, butchered, split or replaced item alike. With no calories on either side
-- (water, salt, makeCoffee) the hunger ratio instBase/scriptHunger with share, and 1 when the script hunger is 0.
function K.vector.instanceScale(vecKcal, cal, frac, instBase, scriptHunger, share)
    if vecKcal ~= nil and vecKcal > 0 and cal ~= nil and cal > 0 then
        return cal * frac / vecKcal, 1
    end
    local scale = 1
    if scriptHunger ~= nil and scriptHunger ~= 0 and instBase ~= nil then
        scale = instBase / scriptHunger
    end
    return scale, share
end

-- vec x scale as a new vector (vec untouched).
function K.vector.scaled(vec, scale)
    local out = K.vector.new()
    K.vector.add(out, vec, scale)
    return out
end
```

```python
def test_a_side_table_edit_makes_the_extract_stale(tmp_path):
    paths = {}
    for name in ("iodine", "phytate", "insects"):
        dst = tmp_path / os.path.basename(fn.REF_SOURCES[name])
        shutil.copyfile(fn.REF_SOURCES[name], dst)
        paths[name] = str(dst)
    assert fn.check_side_tables(paths=paths) == []
    text = open(paths["iodine"], encoding="utf-8", newline="").read().splitlines(True)
    cells = text[1].split(",")
    cells[2] = str(float(cells[2]) + 1.0)                 # the first row's iodine_ug_100g
    text[1] = ",".join(cells)
    open(paths["iodine"], "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="").write("".join(text))
    stale = fn.check_side_tables(paths=paths)
    assert [p for p, _m in stale] == [fn.EXTRACT_JSON]


def test_check_generated_lists_every_stale_file(tmp_path):
    lua_path, script_path = str(tmp_path / "NR_Data_Nutrients.lua"), str(tmp_path / "s.txt")
    infer_path = str(tmp_path / "NR_Data_Infer.lua")
    paths = dict(lua_path=lua_path, script_path=script_path, infer_path=infer_path)
    for path, text in fn.generated_texts(**paths):
        fn.write_text(path, text + "-- edited\n")
    stale = fn.check_generated(build_fresh=False, **paths)
    assert sorted(p for p, _m in stale) == sorted([lua_path, script_path, infer_path])


def test_check_prints_a_refusal_and_exits_1(capsys, monkeypatch):
    def refuse(*a, **k):
        raise fn.BuildRefused("the mapping does not check clean")
    monkeypatch.setattr(fn, "check_generated", refuse)
    assert fn.main(["--check"]) == 1
    assert "food_nutrients: the mapping does not check clean" in capsys.readouterr().err


def test_the_lua_headers_carry_no_date():
    for path, text in fn.generated_texts():
        if path.endswith(".lua"):
            assert not re.search(r"generated \d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}", text.splitlines()[1]), path
```

```python
import pytest


def lst(host, *xs):
    return host.rt.table(*xs)


def tolist(t):
    return [t[i] for i in range(1, len(t) + 1)]


def test_merge_keeps_pending_first_never_doubles_and_drops_the_departed(host):
    out = host.call("sched.merge", lst(host, "a", "b", "c"), 2, lst(host, "a", "c", "d"))
    assert tolist(out) == ["c", "a", "d"]        # b left; c still pending stays first; a re-queued once; d new


def test_merge_with_an_empty_pending_queue_is_the_roster(host):
    assert tolist(host.call("sched.merge", lst(host), 1, lst(host, "x", "y"))) == ["x", "y"]


def test_ticks_per_minute_takes_the_smaller_of_the_last_two(host):
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 7, 6) == 6
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 6, 7) == 6
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 1, 1) == 1            # a fast clock: one tick a minute event (#3348)
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", None, None) == 6      # before two minutes are counted
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 0, None) == 6         # the boot's first minute event: no count yet
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 1, 0) == 1
    assert host.call("sched.ticksPerMinute", 37, None) == 37


def test_the_budget_has_a_15_ms_floor_and_grows_with_the_queue(host):
    assert host.call("sched.budgetMs", 1.6, 6, 25) == 15
    assert host.call("sched.budgetMs", 2.0, 60, 6) == pytest.approx(30.0)    # 2 x 60 / 6 x 1.5
    assert host.call("sched.budgetMs", 1.2, 58, 1) == pytest.approx(104.4)   # a fast clock runs the whole queue


def test_the_run_cap_is_at_least_one(host):
    assert host.call("sched.runCap", 15, 1.6) == 9
    assert host.call("sched.runCap", 15, 40) == 1


def test_the_mean_is_an_ema_with_a_floor(host):
    assert host.call("sched.ema", None, 2.0) == 2.0
    assert host.call("sched.ema", 2.0, 1.0) == pytest.approx(1.8)
    assert host.call("sched.ema", 0.1, 0.0) == pytest.approx(0.1)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `NR_Kernel_Sched.lua`** (one statement per line):

```lua
-- NR_Kernel_Sched.lua -- the slow minute's budgeted queue (Plan 11 Task 9; Decision 6 (b); rules #3497 and #3444):
-- which names run their minute and how many a tick. Pure: lists and numbers in, lists and numbers out.
local K = NutritionRevamp.kernel
K.sched = {}

K.sched.FLOOR_MS = 15 -- #3489, #3490: the smallest fixed cap that starved none at N = 60 at both spacings (Decision 6)
K.sched.HEADROOM = 1.5 -- game choice (Plan 11 ruling 2): 15 ms over the 10 ms that ran at the margin (#3476)
K.sched.SEED_MS = 1.6 -- #3387: one player-run in play cost 1583 us; the mean before any run is timed
K.sched.MIN_MEAN_MS = 0.1 -- game choice: a run timed at 0 ms on the 1 ms clock never drives the cap to infinity
K.sched.EMA_W = 0.2 -- game choice: the weight the harness's budget arm used (ghost.load budget<ms>)
K.sched.TPM_DEFAULT = 6 -- #3346: 6.27 ticks a game minute at DayLength 1, the shortest day measured

-- The pending queue from position head, kept in order for every name still in the roster and never doubled, then
-- every roster name not already queued, in roster order. Returns a new list.
function K.sched.merge(pending, head, roster)
    local out = {}
    local queued = {}
    local live = {}
    for i = 1, #roster do
        live[roster[i]] = true
    end
    for i = head, #pending do
        local u = pending[i]
        if live[u] == true and queued[u] == nil then
            out[#out + 1] = u
            queued[u] = true
        end
    end
    for i = 1, #roster do
        local u = roster[i]
        if queued[u] == nil then
            out[#out + 1] = u
            queued[u] = true
        end
    end
    return out
end

-- The ticks a minute the budget divides by: the smaller of the last two minutes' counts (a 6-tick minute after a
-- 7-tick one starved the drafted spread, #3478); TPM_DEFAULT before a count exists. A count of 0 is no count: every
-- minute event is followed by at least one tick (#3348-#3350), so 0 is only the boot's first minute.
function K.sched.ticksPerMinute(last, prev)
    local t = last
    if t == nil or t < 1 then
        t = K.sched.TPM_DEFAULT
    end
    if prev ~= nil and prev >= 1 and prev < t then
        t = prev
    end
    return t
end

-- The per-tick budget in ms: the minute's work spread over its ticks with headroom, never under the floor. Under a
-- fast clock (one tick a minute event) it is the whole queue's work, so every queued name runs each tick.
function K.sched.budgetMs(meanMs, queued, tpm)
    local need = meanMs * queued / tpm * K.sched.HEADROOM
    return K.max(K.sched.FLOOR_MS, need)
end

-- The most runs a tick may make at the mean cost: at least one, so the queue always moves.
function K.sched.runCap(budgetMs, meanMs)
    local n = math.floor(budgetMs / meanMs)
    if n < 1 then
        return 1
    end
    return n
end

-- The per-run mean, an EMA of each tick's timed cost a run, never under MIN_MEAN_MS.
function K.sched.ema(mean, perRun)
    if mean == nil then
        return K.max(K.sched.MIN_MEAN_MS, perRun)
    end
    return K.max(K.sched.MIN_MEAN_MS, mean + (perRun - mean) * K.sched.EMA_W)
end
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the failing shape tests** `testing/tests/kernel/test_players_shape.py`:

```python
from .server_host import Host

CLOCK = r"""
NR_T.now = 1000000
getTimestampMs = function() return NR_T.now end
"""


def host(per_run_ms=None):
    h = Host(extra_env=CLOCK if per_run_ms is not None else "")
    if per_run_ms is not None:
        h.rt.execute("local P = NutritionRevamp.server.players; local w = P.work; "
                     "P.work = function(u, p) NR_T.now = NR_T.now + %d; return w(u, p) end" % per_run_ms)
    return h


def names(n):
    return ["p%02d" % i for i in range(n)]


def run_minute(h, ticks, minute):
    h.T.age = 100.0 + minute / 60
    h.minute()
    served = []
    for _ in range(ticks):
        d0 = h.NR.server.players.drained
        h.tick(1)
        served.append(h.NR.server.players.drained - d0)
    return served


def test_no_player_starves_when_players_outnumber_ticks():
    h = host(per_run_ms=2)
    h.online(*[h.player(u) for u in names(60)])
    for m in range(1, 6):
        run_minute(h, 6, m)                          # 6 ticks a game minute: DayLength 1 (#3346)
    assert all(h.record(u).lastSeen == 100.0 + 5 / 60 for u in names(60))


def test_a_tick_never_runs_more_than_its_cap():
    h = host(per_run_ms=2)
    h.online(*[h.player(u) for u in names(60)])
    for m in range(1, 4):
        served = run_minute(h, 6, m)
    assert max(served) <= h.NR.server.players.runCap


def test_under_a_fast_clock_everyone_runs_every_tick():
    h = host(per_run_ms=2)
    h.online(*[h.player(u) for u in names(20)])
    for m in range(1, 6):
        run_minute(h, 1, m)                          # one tick a minute event (#3348)
    assert all(h.record(u).lastSeen == 100.0 + 5 / 60 for u in names(20))


def test_unserved_players_are_carried_forward_and_never_doubled():
    h = host()                                       # no clock: the cap is floor(15 / 1.6) = 9 a tick
    h.online(*[h.player(u) for u in names(30)])
    run_minute(h, 1, 1)
    P = h.NR.server.players
    assert P.drained == 9
    pending = [P.queue[i] for i in range(P.queueHead, len(P.queue) + 1)]
    assert len(pending) == 21 and len(set(pending)) == 21
    h.T.age = 100.0 + 2 / 60
    h.minute()                                       # the next minute's merge, before any tick
    merged = [P.queue[i] for i in range(1, len(P.queue) + 1)]
    assert merged[:21] == pending                    # the unserved first, in their order
    assert len(merged) == 30 and len(set(merged)) == 30


def test_a_respawn_inside_a_minute_never_marks_the_new_record_dead():
    h = host()
    a, b = h.player("a"), h.player("b")
    h.online(a, b)
    run_minute(h, 25, 1)
    b.deadFlag = True
    run_minute(h, 25, 2)                             # b's dead body runs: the old record marked dead
    h.T.age = 100.0 + 3 / 60
    h.minute()                                       # b queued with its dead body
    nb = h.player("b")
    h.fire("OnNewGame", nb, None)                    # the respawn before the drain reaches b
    h.tick(3)                                        # the new object enters the list three ticks later (#3358)
    h.online(a, nb)
    h.tick(22)
    assert h.record("b").dead is not True
    run_minute(h, 25, 4)
    assert h.G.rawequal(h.NR.server.players.online["b"], nb)
    assert h.record("b").lastSeen == 100.0 + 4 / 60


def test_a_reconnect_between_two_minutes_fires_first_sight_again():
    h = host()
    seen = h.rt.eval("{}")
    P = h.NR.server.players
    P.onFirstSight[len(P.onFirstSight) + 1] = h.rt.eval("function(t) return function(u) t[#t + 1] = u end end")(seen)
    h.online(h.player("a"))
    run_minute(h, 2, 1)
    h.online(h.player("a"))                          # a new IsoPlayer under the same username (#3360)
    run_minute(h, 2, 2)
    assert [seen[i] for i in range(1, len(seen) + 1)].count("a") == 2


def test_resets_count_respawns_only():
    h = host()
    a = h.player("a")
    h.fire("OnNewGame", a, None)
    assert h.record("a").resets == 0                 # a first character
    h.online(a)
    run_minute(h, 2, 1)
    h.fire("OnNewGame", h.player("a"), None)
    assert h.record("a").resets == 1


def test_a_sixty_game_minute_step_integrates_and_stays_finite():
    h = host()
    h.online(h.player("a"))
    run_minute(h, 2, 1)
    run_minute(h, 2, 61)                             # one hour of game time between two runs
    import math
    r = h.record("a")
    assert r.lastSeen == 100.0 + 61 / 60
    for k in ("fm", "lm", "energyState"):
        assert math.isfinite(r.body[k]), k


def test_one_shot_tasks_run_once_inside_the_queue():
    h = host()
    h.online(h.player("a"))
    hits = h.rt.eval("{ n = 0 }")
    h.NR.server.players.task("t1", h.rt.eval("function(t) return function() t.n = t.n + 1 end end")(hits))
    run_minute(h, 3, 1)
    run_minute(h, 3, 2)
    assert hits.n == 1


def test_the_extra_seam_runs_its_names_every_minute():
    h = host()
    h.online(h.player("a"))
    runs = h.rt.eval("{ n = 0 }")
    h.NR.server.players.extra = h.rt.eval(
        "function(t) return { names = function() return { 'ghost1', 'ghost2' } end, "
        "run = function(name) t.n = t.n + 1 end } end")(runs)
    run_minute(h, 3, 1)
    run_minute(h, 3, 2)
    assert runs.n == 4
```
